I don’t mean to be difficult about it. It’s just that being lighter feels sort of helpful to me—I can get around the garden more easily, and my walks don’t feel as tiring. But yes, I suppose they’re concerned it’s gone down too much.\n\nDoctor: Are you trying to lose weight?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t put it as “trying,” exactly… not in a drastic way. But I suppose I do get a bit uneasy when my wife puts butter on things or gives me a very full plate. I start thinking I’ll put the weight back on and lose the bit of control I’ve managed to keep. I know that may sound silly.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think about your weight or shape when deciding what or how much to eat?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I do, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound vain or anything, but if my belt feels a notch looser, I feel like I’ve done right by myself somehow. And if I catch my stomach looking even a little rounded in the mirror, I feel… sloppy, I guess, and disappointed. I know that may not be the best way to think about it.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I don’t like admitting that, because I know it probably sounds foolish. When my wife puts butter on something, or gives me a really full plate, I do get nervous that I’ll start putting weight back on. It feels like I’d lose the bit of control I’ve worked to keep. Is that… the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating less helps you feel more in control of your life?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, in a way. I wouldn’t say I’m doing anything extreme, but most days I just have coffee and toast in the morning, and maybe some soup or vegetables later on. Big meals just feel a bit unnecessary at my age, if that makes sense. My wife thinks I’m not eating enough, and that’s really why I’m here, but I feel better keeping it simple. Is that all right to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits help you cope with feelings of being overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way, Doctor. I don’t really think, “I’m overwhelmed, so I won’t eat,” if that’s what you mean. It’s more just… keeping meals small feels simpler, less fuss. Maybe that does make things feel a bit steadier, but I haven’t really noticed it as coping, exactly. Is that an acceptable answer?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Most days coffee and a bit of toast in the morning feels fine, and then maybe soup or some vegetables later. Big meals just seem unnecessary to me now, but my wife worries I’m not eating enough.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: A little, I suppose… my doctor did say the number on the scale has gotten too low. I don’t mean to be difficult about it. It’s just that being lighter feels sort of helpful to me—I can get around the garden more easily, and my walks don’t feel as tiring. But yes, I suppose they’re concerned it’s gone down too much.\n\nDoctor: Are you trying to lose weight?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t put it as “trying,” exactly… not in a drastic way. But I suppose I do get a bit uneasy when my wife puts butter on things or gives me a very full plate. I start thinking I’ll put the weight back on and lose the bit of control I’ve managed to keep. I know that may sound silly.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think about your weight or shape when deciding what or how much to eat?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I do, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound vain or anything, but if my belt feels a notch looser, I feel like I’ve done right by myself somehow. And if I catch my stomach looking even a little rounded in the mirror, I feel… sloppy, I guess, and disappointed. I know that may not be the best way to think about it.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I don’t like admitting that, because I know it probably sounds foolish. Is that… the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating less helps you feel more in control of your life?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, in a way. I wouldn’t say I’m doing anything extreme, but most days I just have coffee and toast in the morning, and maybe some soup or vegetables later on. Big meals just feel a bit unnecessary at my age, if that makes sense. My wife thinks I’m not eating enough, and that’s really why I’m here, but I feel better keeping it simple. Is that all right to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits help you cope with feelings of being overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way, Doctor. I don’t really think, “I’m overwhelmed, so I won’t eat,” if that’s what you mean. It’s more just… keeping meals small feels simpler, less fuss. Is that an acceptable answer?\n\nDoctor: Is your main concern when eating that you might gain weight if you eat too much?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably fair to say, though I don’t like making too much of it. It’s mostly when my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a very full plate—I get nervous that if I eat too much I’ll start putting weight back on. And then I feel like I’d lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat more if you weren't worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I suppose I might, yes… if I didn’t have that worry in the back of my mind. Maybe that does make things feel a bit steadier, but I haven’t really noticed it as coping, exactly. Is that an acceptable answer?\n\nDoctor: Is your main concern when eating that you might gain weight if you eat too much?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably fair to say, though I don’t like making too much of it. It’s mostly when my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a very full plate—I get nervous that if I eat too much I’ll start putting weight back on. And then I feel like I’d lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat more if you weren't worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I suppose I might, yes… if I didn’t have that worry in the back of my mind. It’s not that I’m trying to be difficult, Doctor. But when my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a very full plate, I do get nervous that I’ll start putting weight back on and lose the control I’ve worked to keep. So yes, I think I probably would eat a bit more if that didn’t trouble me. Is that a reasonable way to put it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're avoiding certain foods because you're afraid of gaining weight, or is it more that you just don't have an interest in eating them?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more the worry, if I’m being honest… though I don’t mean I’m refusing things just to be awkward. When my wife puts butter on something, or gives me a very full plate, I get nervous that I’ll start putting weight back on. It feels like I’d lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. So yes, I suppose I am avoiding some things because of that fear more than just not being interested. Is that the sort of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Is your decision to eat less mostly because you are afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that is mostly it, Doctor, though I don’t like to make it sound too dramatic. I don’t want to make it sound vain or anything, but if my belt feels a notch looser, I feel like I’ve done right by myself somehow. And if I catch my stomach looking even a little rounded in the mirror, I feel… sloppy, I guess, and disappointed. I know that may not be the best way to think about it.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I don’t like admitting that, because I know it probably sounds foolish. When my wife puts butter on something, or gives me a really full plate, I do get nervous that I’ll start putting weight back on. It feels like I’d lose the bit of control I’ve worked to keep. Is that… the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating less helps you feel more in control of your life?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, in a way. I wouldn’t say I’m doing anything extreme, but most days I just have coffee and toast in the morning, and maybe some soup or vegetables later on. Big meals just feel a bit unnecessary at my age, if that makes sense. My wife thinks I’m not eating enough, and that’s really why I’m here, but I feel better keeping it simple. Is that all right to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits help you cope with feelings of being overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way, Doctor. I don’t really think, “I’m overwhelmed, so I won’t eat,” if that’s what you mean. It’s more just… keeping meals small feels simpler, less fuss. Maybe that does make things feel a bit steadier, but I haven’t really noticed it as coping, exactly. Is that an acceptable answer?\n\nDoctor: Is your main concern when eating that you might gain weight if you eat too much?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably fair to say, though I don’t like making too much of it. It’s mostly when my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a very full plate—I get nervous that if I eat too much I’ll start putting weight back on. And then I feel like I’d lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat more if you weren't worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I suppose I might, yes… if I didn’t have that worry in the back of my mind. It’s not that I’m trying to be difficult, Doctor. But when my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a very full plate, I do get nervous that I’ll start putting weight back on and lose the control I’ve worked to keep. So yes, I think I probably would eat a bit more if that didn’t trouble me. Is that a reasonable way to put it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're avoiding certain foods because you're afraid of gaining weight, or is it more that you just don't have an interest in eating them?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more the worry, if I’m being honest… though I don’t mean I’m refusing things just to be awkward. When my wife puts butter on something, or gives me a very full plate, I get nervous that I’ll start putting weight back on. It feels like I’d lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. So yes, I suppose I am avoiding some things because of that fear more than just not being interested. Is that the sort of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Is your decision to eat less mostly because you are afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that is mostly it, Doctor, though I don’t like to make it sound too dramatic. When my wife tries to put butter on things, or gives me a full plate, I get nervous I’ll start putting weight back on and lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. So… yes, I suppose that’s a fair way to say it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or shape is different from how others see you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I see it quite the way other people do, no… though I don’t want to overstate it. If my belt feels a notch looser, I feel as if I’ve done right by myself somehow. But then if I look in the mirror and my stomach seems even a little rounded, I feel sloppy and disappointed in myself. I suppose that may not be how others would see me. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose… my wife was the one who thought I ought to come in. She feels I’m not eating enough, though I don’t know that it’s all that serious. Most days I’ll just have coffee and a piece of toast in the morning, and maybe some soup or vegetables later on. Big meals just feel a bit unnecessary at my age, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're not hungry most of the time?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m never hungry, exactly… it’s more that I don’t feel the need for much. Most days coffee and a bit of toast in the morning feels fine, and then maybe soup or some vegetables later. Big meals just seem unnecessary to me now, but my wife worries I’m not eating enough.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: A little, I suppose… my doctor did say the number on the scale has gotten too low. I don’t mean to be difficult about it. It’s just that being lighter feels sort of helpful to me—I can get around the garden more easily, and my walks don’t feel as tiring. But yes, I suppose they’re concerned it’s gone down too much.\n\nDoctor: Are you trying to lose weight?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t put it as “trying,” exactly… not in a drastic way. But I suppose I do get a bit uneasy when my wife puts butter on things or gives me a very full plate. I start thinking I’ll put the weight back on and lose the bit of control I’ve managed to keep. I know that may sound silly.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think about your weight or shape when deciding what or how much to eat?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I do, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound vain or anything, but if my belt feels a notch looser, I feel like I’ve done right by myself somehow. And if I catch my stomach looking even a little rounded in the mirror, I feel… sloppy, I guess, and disappointed. I know that may not be the best way to think about it.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I don’t like admitting that, because I know it probably sounds foolish. When my wife puts butter on something, or gives me a really full plate, I do get nervous that I’ll start putting weight back on. It feels like I’d lose the bit of control I’ve worked to keep. Is that… the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating less helps you feel more in control of your life?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, in a way. I wouldn’t say I’m doing anything extreme, but most days I just have coffee and toast in the morning, and maybe some soup or vegetables later on. Big meals just feel a bit unnecessary at my age, if that makes sense. My wife thinks I’m not eating enough, and that’s really why I’m here, but I feel better keeping it simple. Is that all right to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits help you cope with feelings of being overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way, Doctor. I don’t really think, “I’m overwhelmed, so I won’t eat,” if that’s what you mean. It’s more just… keeping meals small feels simpler, less fuss. Maybe that does make things feel a bit steadier, but I haven’t really noticed it as coping, exactly. Is that an acceptable answer?\n\nDoctor: Is your main concern when eating that you might gain weight if you eat too much?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably fair to say, though I don’t like making too much of it. It’s mostly when my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a very full plate—I get nervous that if I eat too much I’ll start putting weight back on. And then I feel like I’d lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat more if you weren't worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I suppose I might, yes… if I didn’t have that worry in the back of my mind. It’s not that I’m trying to be difficult, Doctor. But when my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a very full plate, I do get nervous that I’ll start putting weight back on and lose the control I’ve worked to keep. So yes, I think I probably would eat a bit more if that didn’t trouble me. Is that a reasonable way to put it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're avoiding certain foods because you're afraid of gaining weight, or is it more that you just don't have an interest in eating them?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more the worry, if I’m being honest… though I don’t mean I’m refusing things just to be awkward. When my wife puts butter on something, or gives me a very full plate, I get nervous that I’ll start putting weight back on. It feels like I’d lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. So yes, I suppose I am avoiding some things because of that fear more than just not being interested. Is that the sort of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Is your decision to eat less mostly because you are afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that is mostly it, Doctor, though I don’t like to make it sound too dramatic. When my wife tries to put butter on things, or gives me a full plate, I get nervous I’ll start putting weight back on and lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. So… yes, I suppose that’s a fair way to say it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or shape is different from how others see you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I see it quite the way other people do, no… though I don’t want to overstate it. If my belt feels a notch looser, I feel as if I’ve done right by myself somehow. But then if I look in the mirror and my stomach seems even a little rounded, I feel sloppy and disappointed in myself. I suppose that may not be how others would see me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are underweight or overweight when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I think of myself as underweight, really… maybe that sounds odd. I don’t mean to be difficult about it. It’s just that being lighter feels sort of helpful to me—I can get around the garden more easily, and my walks don’t feel as tiring. But yes, I suppose they’re concerned it’s gone down too much.\n\nDoctor: Are you trying to lose weight?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t put it as “trying,” exactly… not in a drastic way. I don’t want to make it sound vain or anything, but if my belt feels a notch looser, I feel like I’ve done right by myself somehow. I know that may not be the best way to think about it.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I don’t like admitting that, because I know it probably sounds foolish. When my wife puts butter on something, or gives me a really full plate, I do get nervous that I’ll start putting weight back on. It feels like I’d lose the bit of control I’ve worked to keep. Is that… the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating less helps you feel more in control of your life?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, in a way. Is that all right to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits help you cope with feelings of being overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way, Doctor. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose… my wife was the one who thought I ought to come in. She feels I’m not eating enough, though I don’t know that it’s all that serious. Most days I’ll just have coffee and a piece of toast in the morning, and maybe some soup or vegetables later on. Big meals just feel a bit unnecessary at my age, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're not hungry most of the time?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m never hungry, exactly… it’s more that I don’t feel the need for much. Most days coffee and a bit of toast in the morning feels fine, and then maybe soup or some vegetables later. Big meals just seem unnecessary to me now, but my wife worries I’m not eating enough.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: A little, I suppose… my doctor did say the number on the scale has gotten too low. I don’t mean to be difficult about it. It’s just that being lighter feels sort of helpful to me—I can get around the garden more easily, and my walks don’t feel as tiring. But yes, I suppose they’re concerned it’s gone down too much.\n\nDoctor: Are you trying to lose weight?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t put it as “trying,” exactly… not in a drastic way. But I suppose I do get a bit uneasy when my wife puts butter on things or gives me a very full plate. I start thinking I’ll put the weight back on and lose the bit of control I’ve managed to keep. I know that may sound silly.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think about your weight or shape when deciding what or how much to eat?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I do, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound vain or anything, but if my belt feels a notch looser, I feel like I’ve done right by myself somehow. And if I catch my stomach looking even a little rounded in the mirror, I feel… sloppy, I guess, and disappointed. I know that may not be the best way to think about it.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I don’t like admitting that, because I know it probably sounds foolish. When my wife puts butter on something, or gives me a really full plate, I do get nervous that I’ll start putting weight back on. It feels like I’d lose the bit of control I’ve worked to keep. Is that… the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating less helps you feel more in control of your life?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, in a way. I wouldn’t say I’m doing anything extreme, but most days I just have coffee and toast in the morning, and maybe some soup or vegetables later on. Big meals just feel a bit unnecessary at my age, if that makes sense. My wife thinks I’m not eating enough, and that’s really why I’m here, but I feel better keeping it simple. Is that all right to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits help you cope with feelings of being overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way, Doctor. I don’t really think, “I’m overwhelmed, so I won’t eat,” if that’s what you mean. It’s more just… keeping meals small feels simpler, less fuss. Maybe that does make things feel a bit steadier, but I haven’t really noticed it as coping, exactly. Is that an acceptable answer?\n\nDoctor: Is your main concern when eating that you might gain weight if you eat too much?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably fair to say, though I don’t like making too much of it. It’s mostly when my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a very full plate—I get nervous that if I eat too much I’ll start putting weight back on. And then I feel like I’d lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat more if you weren't worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I suppose I might, yes… if I didn’t have that worry in the back of my mind. It’s not that I’m trying to be difficult, Doctor. But when my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a very full plate, I do get nervous that I’ll start putting weight back on and lose the control I’ve worked to keep. So yes, I think I probably would eat a bit more if that didn’t trouble me. Is that a reasonable way to put it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're avoiding certain foods because you're afraid of gaining weight, or is it more that you just don't have an interest in eating them?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more the worry, if I’m being honest… though I don’t mean I’m refusing things just to be awkward. When my wife puts butter on something, or gives me a very full plate, I get nervous that I’ll start putting weight back on. It feels like I’d lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. So yes, I suppose I am avoiding some things because of that fear more than just not being interested. Is that the sort of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Is your decision to eat less mostly because you are afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that is mostly it, Doctor, though I don’t like to make it sound too dramatic. When my wife tries to put butter on things, or gives me a full plate, I get nervous I’ll start putting weight back on and lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. So… yes, I suppose that’s a fair way to say it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or shape is different from how others see you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I see it quite the way other people do, no… though I don’t want to overstate it. If my belt feels a notch looser, I feel as if I’ve done right by myself somehow. But then if I look in the mirror and my stomach seems even a little rounded, I feel sloppy and disappointed in myself. I suppose that may not be how others would see me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are underweight or overweight when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I think of myself as underweight, really… maybe that sounds odd. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose… my wife was the one who thought I ought to come in. She feels I’m not eating enough, though I don’t know that it’s all that serious. Most days I’ll just have coffee and a piece of toast in the morning, and maybe some soup or vegetables later on. Big meals just feel a bit unnecessary at my age, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're not hungry most of the time?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m never hungry, exactly… it’s more that I don’t feel the need for much. Most days coffee and a bit of toast in the morning feels fine, and then maybe soup or some vegetables later. Big meals just seem unnecessary to me now, but my wife worries I’m not eating enough.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: A little, I suppose… my doctor did say the number on the scale has gotten too low. I don’t mean to be difficult about it. It’s just that being lighter feels sort of helpful to me—I can get around the garden more easily, and my walks don’t feel as tiring. But yes, I suppose they’re concerned it’s gone down too much.\n\nDoctor: Are you trying to lose weight?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t put it as “trying,” exactly… not in a drastic way. But I suppose I do get a bit uneasy when my wife puts butter on things or gives me a very full plate. I start thinking I’ll put the weight back on and lose the bit of control I’ve managed to keep. I know that may sound silly.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think about your weight or shape when deciding what or how much to eat?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I do, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound vain or anything, but if my belt feels a notch looser, I feel like I’ve done right by myself somehow. And if I catch my stomach looking even a little rounded in the mirror, I feel… sloppy, I guess, and disappointed. I know that may not be the best way to think about it.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I don’t like admitting that, because I know it probably sounds foolish. When my wife puts butter on something, or gives me a really full plate, I do get nervous that I’ll start putting weight back on. It feels like I’d lose the bit of control I’ve worked to keep. Is that… the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating less helps you feel more in control of your life?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, in a way. I wouldn’t say I’m doing anything extreme, but most days I just have coffee and toast in the morning, and maybe some soup or vegetables later on. Big meals just feel a bit unnecessary at my age, if that makes sense. My wife thinks I’m not eating enough, and that’s really why I’m here, but I feel better keeping it simple. Is that all right to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits help you cope with feelings of being overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way, Doctor. I don’t really think, “I’m overwhelmed, so I won’t eat,” if that’s what you mean. It’s more just… keeping meals small feels simpler, less fuss. Maybe that does make things feel a bit steadier, but I haven’t really noticed it as coping, exactly. Is that an acceptable answer?\n\nDoctor: Is your main concern when eating that you might gain weight if you eat too much?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably fair to say, though I don’t like making too much of it. It’s mostly when my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a very full plate—I get nervous that if I eat too much I’ll start putting weight back on. And then I feel like I’d lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat more if you weren't worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I suppose I might, yes… if I didn’t have that worry in the back of my mind. It’s not that I’m trying to be difficult, Doctor. But when my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a very full plate, I do get nervous that I’ll start putting weight back on and lose the control I’ve worked to keep. So yes, I think I probably would eat a bit more if that didn’t trouble me. Is that a reasonable way to put it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're avoiding certain foods because you're afraid of gaining weight, or is it more that you just don't have an interest in eating them?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more the worry, if I’m being honest… though I don’t mean I’m refusing things just to be awkward. When my wife puts butter on something, or gives me a very full plate, I get nervous that I’ll start putting weight back on. It feels like I’d lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. So yes, I suppose I am avoiding some things because of that fear more than just not being interested. Is that the sort of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Is your decision to eat less mostly because you are afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that is mostly it, Doctor, though I don’t like to make it sound too dramatic. When my wife tries to put butter on things, or gives me a full plate, I get nervous I’ll start putting weight back on and lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. So… yes, I suppose that’s a fair way to say it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or shape is different from how others see you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I see it quite the way other people do, no… though I don’t want to overstate it. If my belt feels a notch looser, I feel as if I’ve done right by myself somehow. But then if I look in the mirror and my stomach seems even a little rounded, I feel sloppy and disappointed in myself. I suppose that may not be how others would see me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are underweight or overweight when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I think of myself as underweight, really… maybe that sounds odd. I suppose that’s probably not the most sensible way to judge it, is it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is lower than what is healthy for someone of your age and height?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really see it that way, Doctor. Maybe I’m missing something, but I don’t quite understand why there’s such a fuss.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight the main reason you limit your food intake?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s the main reason, exactly… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Well… I suppose I don’t quite see what they’re seeing, if I’m honest. Everyone keeps saying I’m underweight, but I feel mostly normal for my age. Older people don’t need as much food, do they? What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose… my wife was the one who thought I ought to come in. She feels I’m not eating enough, though I don’t know that it’s all that serious. Most days I’ll just have coffee and a piece of toast in the morning, and maybe some soup or vegetables later on. Big meals just feel a bit unnecessary at my age, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're not hungry most of the time?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m never hungry, exactly… it’s more that I don’t feel the need for much. Most days coffee and a bit of toast in the morning feels fine, and then maybe soup or some vegetables later. Big meals just seem unnecessary to me now, but my wife worries I’m not eating enough.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: A little, I suppose… my doctor did say the number on the scale has gotten too low. I don’t mean to be difficult about it. It’s just that being lighter feels sort of helpful to me—I can get around the garden more easily, and my walks don’t feel as tiring. But yes, I suppose they’re concerned it’s gone down too much.\n\nDoctor: Are you trying to lose weight?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t put it as “trying,” exactly… not in a drastic way. But I suppose I do get a bit uneasy when my wife puts butter on things or gives me a very full plate. I start thinking I’ll put the weight back on and lose the bit of control I’ve managed to keep. I know that may sound silly.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think about your weight or shape when deciding what or how much to eat?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I do, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound vain or anything, but if my belt feels a notch looser, I feel like I’ve done right by myself somehow. And if I catch my stomach looking even a little rounded in the mirror, I feel… sloppy, I guess, and disappointed. I know that may not be the best way to think about it.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I don’t like admitting that, because I know it probably sounds foolish. When my wife puts butter on something, or gives me a really full plate, I do get nervous that I’ll start putting weight back on. It feels like I’d lose the bit of control I’ve worked to keep. Is that… the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating less helps you feel more in control of your life?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, in a way. I wouldn’t say I’m doing anything extreme, but most days I just have coffee and toast in the morning, and maybe some soup or vegetables later on. Big meals just feel a bit unnecessary at my age, if that makes sense. My wife thinks I’m not eating enough, and that’s really why I’m here, but I feel better keeping it simple. Is that all right to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits help you cope with feelings of being overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way, Doctor. I don’t really think, “I’m overwhelmed, so I won’t eat,” if that’s what you mean. It’s more just… keeping meals small feels simpler, less fuss. Maybe that does make things feel a bit steadier, but I haven’t really noticed it as coping, exactly. Is that an acceptable answer?\n\nDoctor: Is your main concern when eating that you might gain weight if you eat too much?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably fair to say, though I don’t like making too much of it. It’s mostly when my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a very full plate—I get nervous that if I eat too much I’ll start putting weight back on. And then I feel like I’d lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat more if you weren't worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I suppose I might, yes… if I didn’t have that worry in the back of my mind. It’s not that I’m trying to be difficult, Doctor. But when my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a very full plate, I do get nervous that I’ll start putting weight back on and lose the control I’ve worked to keep. So yes, I think I probably would eat a bit more if that didn’t trouble me. Is that a reasonable way to put it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're avoiding certain foods because you're afraid of gaining weight, or is it more that you just don't have an interest in eating them?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more the worry, if I’m being honest… though I don’t mean I’m refusing things just to be awkward. When my wife puts butter on something, or gives me a very full plate, I get nervous that I’ll start putting weight back on. It feels like I’d lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. So yes, I suppose I am avoiding some things because of that fear more than just not being interested. Is that the sort of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Is your decision to eat less mostly because you are afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that is mostly it, Doctor, though I don’t like to make it sound too dramatic. When my wife tries to put butter on things, or gives me a full plate, I get nervous I’ll start putting weight back on and lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. So… yes, I suppose that’s a fair way to say it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or shape is different from how others see you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I see it quite the way other people do, no… though I don’t want to overstate it. If my belt feels a notch looser, I feel as if I’ve done right by myself somehow. But then if I look in the mirror and my stomach seems even a little rounded, I feel sloppy and disappointed in myself. I suppose that may not be how others would see me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are underweight or overweight when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I think of myself as underweight, really… maybe that sounds odd. I know other people might see me differently. When I look in the mirror, if my stomach looks even a little rounded, I feel rather sloppy, like I’ve let myself down. But if my belt is a bit looser, I feel like I’ve done the right thing. I suppose that’s probably not the most sensible way to judge it, is it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is lower than what is healthy for someone of your age and height?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really see it that way, Doctor. I know everyone seems a bit concerned, and I don’t want to be difficult, but at my age people do eat less, don’t they? I’m still getting my chores done and going on my tours, so it doesn’t feel to me like it’s unhealthy exactly. Maybe I’m missing something, but I don’t quite understand why there’s such a fuss.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight the main reason you limit your food intake?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s the main reason, exactly… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But, yes, when my wife puts butter on things or gives me a full plate, I do get a bit nervous. I start thinking I’ll put weight back on and lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight the only reason you eat less than you probably should?\n\nPatient: No, I suppose it’s not the only reason. I don’t mean to be awkward about it, Doctor… it’s just that big meals feel a bit unnecessary at my age. I don’t feel like I’m starving myself, but… maybe it does sound a bit little when I say it out loud.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight the main reason you eat the way you do?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, it’s a big part of it, though I don’t like admitting that. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose… my wife was the one who thought I ought to come in. She feels I’m not eating enough, though I don’t know that it’s all that serious. Most days I’ll just have coffee and a piece of toast in the morning, and maybe some soup or vegetables later on. Big meals just feel a bit unnecessary at my age, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're not hungry most of the time?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I’m never hungry, exactly… it’s more that I don’t feel the need for much. Most days coffee and a bit of toast in the morning feels fine, and then maybe soup or some vegetables later. Big meals just seem unnecessary to me now, but my wife worries I’m not eating enough.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: A little, I suppose… my doctor did say the number on the scale has gotten too low. I don’t mean to be difficult about it. It’s just that being lighter feels sort of helpful to me—I can get around the garden more easily, and my walks don’t feel as tiring. But yes, I suppose they’re concerned it’s gone down too much.\n\nDoctor: Are you trying to lose weight?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t put it as “trying,” exactly… not in a drastic way. But I suppose I do get a bit uneasy when my wife puts butter on things or gives me a very full plate. I start thinking I’ll put the weight back on and lose the bit of control I’ve managed to keep. I know that may sound silly.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think about your weight or shape when deciding what or how much to eat?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I do, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound vain or anything, but if my belt feels a notch looser, I feel like I’ve done right by myself somehow. And if I catch my stomach looking even a little rounded in the mirror, I feel… sloppy, I guess, and disappointed. I know that may not be the best way to think about it.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I don’t like admitting that, because I know it probably sounds foolish. When my wife puts butter on something, or gives me a really full plate, I do get nervous that I’ll start putting weight back on. It feels like I’d lose the bit of control I’ve worked to keep. Is that… the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating less helps you feel more in control of your life?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, in a way. I wouldn’t say I’m doing anything extreme, but most days I just have coffee and toast in the morning, and maybe some soup or vegetables later on. Big meals just feel a bit unnecessary at my age, if that makes sense. My wife thinks I’m not eating enough, and that’s really why I’m here, but I feel better keeping it simple. Is that all right to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits help you cope with feelings of being overwhelmed?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it that way, Doctor. I don’t really think, “I’m overwhelmed, so I won’t eat,” if that’s what you mean. It’s more just… keeping meals small feels simpler, less fuss. Maybe that does make things feel a bit steadier, but I haven’t really noticed it as coping, exactly. Is that an acceptable answer?\n\nDoctor: Is your main concern when eating that you might gain weight if you eat too much?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably fair to say, though I don’t like making too much of it. It’s mostly when my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a very full plate—I get nervous that if I eat too much I’ll start putting weight back on. And then I feel like I’d lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would eat more if you weren't worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I suppose I might, yes… if I didn’t have that worry in the back of my mind. It’s not that I’m trying to be difficult, Doctor. But when my wife puts butter on things, or gives me a very full plate, I do get nervous that I’ll start putting weight back on and lose the control I’ve worked to keep. So yes, I think I probably would eat a bit more if that didn’t trouble me. Is that a reasonable way to put it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're avoiding certain foods because you're afraid of gaining weight, or is it more that you just don't have an interest in eating them?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more the worry, if I’m being honest… though I don’t mean I’m refusing things just to be awkward. When my wife puts butter on something, or gives me a very full plate, I get nervous that I’ll start putting weight back on. It feels like I’d lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. So yes, I suppose I am avoiding some things because of that fear more than just not being interested. Is that the sort of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Is your decision to eat less mostly because you are afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that is mostly it, Doctor, though I don’t like to make it sound too dramatic. When my wife tries to put butter on things, or gives me a full plate, I get nervous I’ll start putting weight back on and lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. So… yes, I suppose that’s a fair way to say it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or shape is different from how others see you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I see it quite the way other people do, no… though I don’t want to overstate it. If my belt feels a notch looser, I feel as if I’ve done right by myself somehow. But then if I look in the mirror and my stomach seems even a little rounded, I feel sloppy and disappointed in myself. I suppose that may not be how others would see me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are underweight or overweight when you look in the mirror?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say I think of myself as underweight, really… maybe that sounds odd. I know other people might see me differently. When I look in the mirror, if my stomach looks even a little rounded, I feel rather sloppy, like I’ve let myself down. But if my belt is a bit looser, I feel like I’ve done the right thing. I suppose that’s probably not the most sensible way to judge it, is it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight is lower than what is healthy for someone of your age and height?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really see it that way, Doctor. I know everyone seems a bit concerned, and I don’t want to be difficult, but at my age people do eat less, don’t they? I’m still getting my chores done and going on my tours, so it doesn’t feel to me like it’s unhealthy exactly. Maybe I’m missing something, but I don’t quite understand why there’s such a fuss.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight the main reason you limit your food intake?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s the main reason, exactly… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But, yes, when my wife puts butter on things or gives me a full plate, I do get a bit nervous. I start thinking I’ll put weight back on and lose the control I’ve been trying to keep. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight the only reason you eat less than you probably should?\n\nPatient: No, I suppose it’s not the only reason. I don’t mean to be awkward about it, Doctor… it’s just that big meals feel a bit unnecessary at my age. Most days I’ll have coffee and toast in the morning, and then maybe some soup or vegetables later on. My wife thinks I’m not eating enough, and that’s probably why I’m here, really. I don’t feel like I’m starving myself, but… maybe it does sound a bit little when I say it out loud.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight the main reason you eat the way you do?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, it’s a big part of it, though I don’t like admitting that. When my wife puts butter on things or gives me a full plate, I get quite uneasy, like if I start eating that way I’ll put the weight back on and lose the control I’ve worked hard to keep. I know that probably sounds a bit silly, but that’s what goes through my mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape is normal when others tell you that you are underweight?\n\nPatient: Well… I suppose I don’t quite see what they’re seeing, if I’m honest. Everyone keeps saying I’m underweight, but I feel mostly normal for my age. Older people don’t need as much food, do they? And I’m still getting around, doing my chores and the tours and all that, so it’s hard for me to understand why everyone is making such a fuss. Maybe I’m wrong, Doctor, but that’s how it seems to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your fear of gaining weight is because you believe you would look unattractive if you gained weight?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t put it quite as strongly as “unattractive,” Doctor… that sounds a bit vain, doesn’t it?
